From mtang at stanford.edu  Tue Jul  7 13:54:35 2020
From: mtang at stanford.edu (Mary Tang)
Date: Tue, 7 Jul 2020 13:54:35 -0700
Subject: [snf-promcommittee] PROM for Flex9H from Solip Tech
In-Reply-To: <CA+eheBj-j6Fb4_5H5FBo9=TP9nNgcLkhbFGDsAh10Chk+pW8eg@mail.gmail.com>
References: <CA+eheBhP-ZEkH8mNB2NAPFGcGxerMq_Y-0Scxmv5gPymVtB=Zw@mail.gmail.com>
 <CA+eheBj-j6Fb4_5H5FBo9=TP9nNgcLkhbFGDsAh10Chk+pW8eg@mail.gmail.com>
Message-ID: <ff558ca8-cdeb-c10c-a8a8-41e8ea9c5539@stanford.edu>

Hi PROMmers -

Sorry, I? haven't weighed in....? My two cents', if it is not too late:

Normally, I'd think Amit's requests are fine in litho.? However, I'm not 
sure when it comes to the Micromist system because basically, we have a 
high voltage placed across what I think is a combustible aerosol.? 
Actually, it's probably as bad as that sounds, because:

1. The volume is small (a few ml?)

2.? The solutions are typically diluted a lot, with a 
not-very-combustible solvent (NMP)

3.? The off-spray should be small.

Looking at the SDS's:

- The Flex9H has a concentration of MIBK that is 10-30%.? The flash 
point of MIBK is 14 C.

- The PRM2150 primer has an ethanol concentration of 25-35% (flashpoint 
of ethanol is 13 C).

I would suggest:

1.? The primer should be spun on the headway for several reasons.? 
First, it's easier.? Second, the primer also has 50-60% water.? As I 
recall, the fellow who invented this thing has said that water doesn't 
electrospray very well.

2.? Let's find out what, if any, dilution Amit plans to do and with 
which solvent.? I would imagine that with sufficient dilution with NMP, 
we could keep the concentration to below the Lower-explosion-limit. It 
would also be a good idea to consult with Mitch's expert contacts on 
recommendations for starting points and addressing any possible risks.

I don't want to say "no" because this would be an interesting experiment 
and a good use of this system.? But we should make sure it is operated 
within recommendations of the manufacturer.

If Amit wants to experiment with conventional resists, I think this 
should be fine as this is documented (where solvents aren't nearly as 
flammable).

What does everyone else think?

Mary

Hi Mary,

On Tue, Jul 7, 2020 at 1:54 PM Mary Tang <mtang at stanford.edu<mailto:mtang at stanford.edu>> wrote:

Hi PROMmers -

Sorry, I  haven't weighed in....  My two cents', if it is not too late:

Normally, I'd think Amit's requests are fine in litho.  However, I'm not sure when it comes to the Micromist system because basically, we have a high voltage placed across what I think is a combustible aerosol.  Actually, it's probably as bad as that sounds, because:

1. The volume is small (a few ml?)

2.  The solutions are typically diluted a lot, with a not-very-combustible solvent (NMP)

3.  The off-spray should be small.

Looking at the SDS's:

- The Flex9H has a concentration of MIBK that is 10-30%.  The flash point of MIBK is 14 C.

- The PRM2150 primer has an ethanol concentration of 25-35% (flashpoint of ethanol is 13 C).

I would suggest:

1.  The primer should be spun on the headway for several reasons.  First, it's easier.  Second, the primer also has 50-60% water.  As I recall, the fellow who invented this thing has said that water doesn't electrospray very well.

2.  Let's find out what, if any, dilution Amit plans to do and with which solvent.  I would imagine that with sufficient dilution with NMP, we could keep the concentration to below the Lower-explosion-limit. It would also be a good idea to consult with Mitch's expert contacts on recommendations for starting points and addressing any possible risks.

I don't want to say "no" because this would be an interesting experiment and a good use of this system.  But we should make sure it is operated within recommendations of the manufacturer.

If Amit wants to experiment with conventional resists, I think this should be fine as this is documented (where solvents aren't nearly as flammable).

What does everyone else think?

Mary
On 7/7/2020 10:11 AM, Amit Gupta wrote:
Dear PROM Committee,

I was wondering about the status of the PROM application for Flex9H and PRM2150.  Please let me know if there are any questions or issues with the PROM application.

Looking forward to hearing from you.

On Thu, Jun 25, 2020 at 11:44 AM Amit Gupta <agupta66 at gmail.com<mailto:agupta66 at gmail.com>> wrote:
Dear SNF PROM Committee,

I hope this email finds you all well.

As the lab is planned to be opened in the near future, I was writing to request the usage of a material called Flex9H from Solip Tech inside the fab.  It is a Siloxane based resin.  We will primarily be coating the material with the micromist coater, but may on occasion use headway2.

We will not be storing the chemical in the fab at this time, but may request for the storage in the future depending on the usage.

Please find attached the PROM form, data sheet and the MSDS for the material.

Please let me know if you have any questions or concerns.

--

Best regards,
Amit Gupta

Best regards,
Amit Gupta

From michelle.rincon at gmail.com  Tue Jul  7 17:11:56 2020
From: michelle.rincon at gmail.com (Michelle Rincon)
Date: Tue, 7 Jul 2020 17:11:56 -0700
Subject: [snf-promcommittee] PROM for Flex9H from Solip Tech
In-Reply-To: <CA+eheBhP-ZEkH8mNB2NAPFGcGxerMq_Y-0Scxmv5gPymVtB=Zw@mail.gmail.com>
References: <CA+eheBhP-ZEkH8mNB2NAPFGcGxerMq_Y-0Scxmv5gPymVtB=Zw@mail.gmail.com>
Message-ID: <CAFSuyDTrEyDbv99nEHF0grYfpvjeoyHLzk1pSk7mVFh8R+YZsA@mail.gmail.com>

Hi Amit,

Thank you for your request! I hope things are going well for you as you get
back into the fab.

The headway should be fine, but we have concerns about your requests for
use in the Mister Mister. It appears that you will be spraying a flammable
aerosol into the high voltage system, based on the flashpoints in the SDS's
you provided. Are you planning to dilute the materials at all? If so, how
much and with what solvent?

Swaroop will also check with Mitch to see if there are tips about working
with these types of materials in the spray coater.

Thanks,
Michelle

From agupta66 at gmail.com  Thu Jul  9 08:54:25 2020
From: agupta66 at gmail.com (Amit Gupta)
Date: Thu, 9 Jul 2020 08:54:25 -0700
Subject: [snf-promcommittee] PROM for Flex9H from Solip Tech
In-Reply-To: <CA+eheBjp5RydOFFzYOknDUkz9tYUJVJQANn02CD93Oe6XBrfQQ@mail.gmail.com>
References: <CA+eheBhP-ZEkH8mNB2NAPFGcGxerMq_Y-0Scxmv5gPymVtB=Zw@mail.gmail.com>
 <CAFSuyDTrEyDbv99nEHF0grYfpvjeoyHLzk1pSk7mVFh8R+YZsA@mail.gmail.com>
 <CA+eheBjp5RydOFFzYOknDUkz9tYUJVJQANn02CD93Oe6XBrfQQ@mail.gmail.com>
Message-ID: <CA+eheBhKBe=-HFAwTLqBk+fiE=gjr5Q=1BEJEUuT_m=WgqErxA@mail.gmail.com>

Hi Michelle and SNF PROM Committee,

I would like to propose a change to the flow process to address the safety
concern of using Solip Flex9H on the micromist coater.

I see that the primer, Solip PRM 2150, is non-flammable from the MSDS and
so I think it should be okay to use with the micromist coater.  As our test
substrates are extremely thin and fragile, we cannot use them on headway
for coating.  We will use headway only as a reference on thick samples.

Below is the process we propose:

1. Coat PRM2150 using micromist coater or headway (I have attached the
updated PROM application for Solip PRM2150).
2. Softbake 40-50C on hotplate
3. Coat Solip Flex9H using hand held wire coater or headway.  (I have
attached the updated PROM application for Solip Flex9H).
4. Complete the curing process.

I hope this is an acceptable process flow. Please let me know if there are
any questions on this.

In the event, Mitch replies that we are okay to use Solip Flex9H on the
micromist coater, I was hoping to still pursue it as an option.

Please let me know if there are any questions on this.  Looking forward to
your response on this.

Best,
Amit

> Dear Yujo and Wilman,

> As far as I know there is no chamber clean process available for CeO2.
>   If the chamber is contaminated with metal oxides it is mechanically
> cleaned by external vendor.
>
> We often done depositions of multi-component metal oxide films in the same
> chamber and don?t see contamination from element to element by XRF when
> using thermal ALD.
>
> On the other hand, we don?t use the same chamber for deposition of LowK
> films and metal oxide films.  Traces of metal contamination will impact
> dielectric quality of SiO2 films deposited in the same chamber.  Thus,
> other teams may see trace contamination with Ce or Hf if the same chamber
> is used interchangeably to deposit SiO2 for accurate dielectric
> measurements.

> is disassembled and cleaned.  You may consider to run TMA before your
> HfO2/CeO2 project to ?passivate? the chamber from prior depositions.

Begin forwarded message:

From: "Ivanov, Sergei V." <Sergei.Ivanov at versummaterials.com<mailto:Sergei.Ivanov at versummaterials.com>>
Subject: RE: Ce(iPrCp)3 contamination concern?
Date: June 9, 2020 at 04:28:36 PDT
To: Wilman Tsai <wtsaia at stanford.edu<mailto:wtsaia at stanford.edu>>, Yujo Zhouchangwan Yu <zyu21 at stanford.edu<mailto:zyu21 at stanford.edu>>, Mansour Moinpour <mansour.moinpour at emdgroup.com<mailto:mansour.moinpour at emdgroup.com>>

Dear Yujo and Wilman,

As far as I know there is no chamber clean process available for CeO2.   If the chamber is contaminated with metal oxides it is mechanically cleaned by external vendor.

We often done depositions of multi-component metal oxide films in the same chamber and don?t see contamination from element to element by XRF when using thermal ALD.

On the other hand, we don?t use the same chamber for deposition of LowK films and metal oxide films.  Traces of metal contamination will impact dielectric quality of SiO2 films deposited in the same chamber.  Thus, other teams may see trace contamination with Ce or Hf if the same chamber is used interchangeably to deposit SiO2 for accurate dielectric measurements.

I don?t see any issues if the chamber is dedicated to deposition of metal oxide films.  You still have to be careful to avoid chemical condensation in the lines and on the chamber walls.   Essentially, avoid any cold spots.  This is important for any chemical with low vapor pressure.

When we switch from metal oxide deposition project (not form wafer to wafer) we deposit 50-100 nm of aluminum oxide from TMA and make sure that TMA process matches baseline.  If we can?t match the baseline, the chamber is disassembled and cleaned.  You may consider to run TMA before your HfO2/CeO2 project to ?passivate? the chamber from prior depositions.

Hopefully this is helpful.

Best regards,
Sergei





On Jun 26, 2020, at 11:46, Michelle Rincon <michelle.rincon at gmail.com<mailto:michelle.rincon at gmail.com>> wrote:

Hi Yujo,

Thanks for this! I have a couple of notes- could you please elaborate more on where the 100 cycles of HfO2 suggestion came from? You can include details from your discussion with the vendor, but we should have it documented in such a way that it will make sense to us a few years from now when we try to remember why we chose that. Also- sending hazardous materials back is often quite difficult for us- does the vendor need the unused material back when you are done with your work?

Best,
Michelle

On Thu, Jul 9, 2020 at 8:54 AM Amit Gupta <agupta66 at gmail.com<mailto:agupta66 at gmail.com>> wrote:
Hi Michelle and SNF PROM Committee,

I would like to propose a change to the flow process to address the safety concern of using Solip Flex9H on the micromist coater.

I see that the primer, Solip PRM 2150, is non-flammable from the MSDS and so I think it should be okay to use with the micromist coater.  As our test substrates are extremely thin and fragile, we cannot use them on headway for coating.  We will use headway only as a reference on thick samples.

Below is the process we propose:

1. Coat PRM2150 using micromist coater or headway (I have attached the updated PROM application for Solip PRM2150).
2. Softbake 40-50C on hotplate
3. Coat Solip Flex9H using hand held wire coater or headway.  (I have attached the updated PROM application for Solip Flex9H).
4. Complete the curing process.

I hope this is an acceptable process flow. Please let me know if there are any questions on this.

In the event, Mitch replies that we are okay to use Solip Flex9H on the micromist coater, I was hoping to still pursue it as an option.

Please let me know if there are any questions on this.  Looking forward to your response on this.

Best,
Amit

On Wed, Jul 8, 2020 at 7:31 AM Amit Gupta <agupta66 at gmail.com<mailto:agupta66 at gmail.com>> wrote:
Hi Michelle,

Thank you for the update.  Yes, I see your point on the flammability of the solution.  I will talk to vendor about this issue and get back to you as soon as possible.

Best,
Amit

On Tue, Jul 7, 2020 at 5:12 PM Michelle Rincon <michelle.rincon at gmail.com<mailto:michelle.rincon at gmail.com>> wrote:
Hi Amit,

Thank you for your request! I hope things are going well for you as you get back into the fab.

The headway should be fine, but we have concerns about your requests for use in the Mister Mister. It appears that you will be spraying a flammable aerosol into the high voltage system, based on the flashpoints in the SDS's you provided. Are you planning to dilute the materials at all? If so, how much and with what solvent?

Swaroop will also check with Mitch to see if there are tips about working with these types of materials in the spray coater.

Thanks,
Michelle

On Thu, Jun 25, 2020 at 11:44 AM Amit Gupta <agupta66 at gmail.com<mailto:agupta66 at gmail.com>> wrote:
Dear SNF PROM Committee,

I hope this email finds you all well.

As the lab is planned to be opened in the near future, I was writing to request the usage of a material called Flex9H from Solip Tech inside the fab.  It is a Siloxane based resin.  We will primarily be coating the material with the micromist coater, but may on occasion use headway2.

We will not be storing the chemical in the fab at this time, but may request for the storage in the future depending on the usage.

Please find attached the PROM form, data sheet and the MSDS for the material.

Please let me know if you have any questions or concerns.

Hi Michelle, Prom Committee,
      Not sure whether handheld wire coaters will work for 2150 or for Flex9H for that matter. He would have to do it in a wet bench/fumehood and would have to wear the appropriate PPE. I would guess that viscosity is probably one of the parameters for a good coat for the wire coaters. The viscosity of Solip Flex 9H is 200+/- 100 cps from the technical data sheet. I couldn?t find viscosity information for 2150. It is probably lower and might behave differently than Solip Flex9H.

The experiment of mixing 2150 with NMP and trying to spray it using Micromist coater is probably OK. We would want to let Amit know to make sure to clean the micromist coater when done with IPA (any suggestions for a solvent for cleaning?) and cleanroom wipes and to collect the waste.

I am not very familiar with these chemistries and would welcome any suggestions from the team.

Thanks,
Swaroop

Thanks Swaroop! Do you know what he means when he says he would use a hand held wire coater? Is that something that might also work for the 2150? Do you think there might be a risk to creating a big mess or damaging the equipment if he experiments with mixing with NMP to get a good spray?

Michelle

On Fri, Jul 10, 2020 at 1:24 PM Swaroop Kommera <skommera at stanford.edu<mailto:skommera at stanford.edu>> wrote:

     Mitch checked in with NTE.  They don?t seem to recommend spray coating Solip Flex9H due to the lower flash point.

On Thu, Jul 9, 2020 at 8:54 AM Amit Gupta <agupta66 at gmail.com<mailto:agupta66 at gmail.com>> wrote:
Hi Michelle and SNF PROM Committee,

I would like to propose a change to the flow process to address the safety concern of using Solip Flex9H on the micromist coater.

I see that the primer, Solip PRM 2150, is non-flammable from the MSDS and so I think it should be okay to use with the micromist coater.  As our test substrates are extremely thin and fragile, we cannot use them on headway for coating.  We will use headway only as a reference on thick samples.

Below is the process we propose:

1. Coat PRM2150 using micromist coater or headway (I have attached the updated PROM application for Solip PRM2150).
2. Softbake 40-50C on hotplate
3. Coat Solip Flex9H using hand held wire coater or headway.  (I have attached the updated PROM application for Solip Flex9H).
4. Complete the curing process.

I hope this is an acceptable process flow. Please let me know if there are any questions on this.

In the event, Mitch replies that we are okay to use Solip Flex9H on the micromist coater, I was hoping to still pursue it as an option.

Please let me know if there are any questions on this.  Looking forward to your response on this.

Best,
Amit

On Wed, Jul 8, 2020 at 7:31 AM Amit Gupta <agupta66 at gmail.com<mailto:agupta66 at gmail.com>> wrote:
Hi Michelle,

Thank you for the update.  Yes, I see your point on the flammability of the solution.  I will talk to vendor about this issue and get back to you as soon as possible.

Best,
Amit

On Tue, Jul 7, 2020 at 5:12 PM Michelle Rincon <michelle.rincon at gmail.com<mailto:michelle.rincon at gmail.com>> wrote:
Hi Amit,

Thank you for your request! I hope things are going well for you as you get back into the fab.

The headway should be fine, but we have concerns about your requests for use in the Mister Mister. It appears that you will be spraying a flammable aerosol into the high voltage system, based on the flashpoints in the SDS's you provided. Are you planning to dilute the materials at all? If so, how much and with what solvent?

Swaroop will also check with Mitch to see if there are tips about working with these types of materials in the spray coater.

Thanks,
Michelle

On Thu, Jun 25, 2020 at 11:44 AM Amit Gupta <agupta66 at gmail.com<mailto:agupta66 at gmail.com>> wrote:
Dear SNF PROM Committee,

I hope this email finds you all well.

As the lab is planned to be opened in the near future, I was writing to request the usage of a material called Flex9H from Solip Tech inside the fab.  It is a Siloxane based resin.  We will primarily be coating the material with the micromist coater, but may on occasion use headway2.

We will not be storing the chemical in the fab at this time, but may request for the storage in the future depending on the usage.

Please find attached the PROM form, data sheet and the MSDS for the material.

Please let me know if you have any questions or concerns.

--

Best regards,
Amit Gupta

Hi Amit,

Thank you for your patience!

We have some follow up questions for you:
1. As far as the Solip Flex9H:
-NTE does not recommend using the mist coater, so we are not going to allow
it.
-Your proposal for the Headway is approved.
-As for the wire coating, have you done this procedure before? Where would
you propose to do it? It definitely would fit within the safety
specifications, but we should work out the logistics.

2. For the 2150,
- Mitch and NTE do have some concerns that it won't spray well since it is
water based. You may need to try mixing it with NMP to see if that might
help. You are approved to try that on the mister if you wish, but you will
need to make sure that you very carefully and thoroughly clean the coater
with IPA when you are finished and to collect the cleanroom wipes
separately for disposal.
- Your request for the headway is approved.
- Do you think the wire coater might work for this too?

Best regards,
Michelle

On Thu, Jul 9, 2020 at 8:54 AM Amit Gupta <agupta66 at gmail.com> wrote:

> Hi Michelle and SNF PROM Committee,
>
> I would like to propose a change to the flow process to address the safety
> concern of using Solip Flex9H on the micromist coater.
>
> I see that the primer, Solip PRM 2150, is non-flammable from the MSDS and
> so I think it should be okay to use with the micromist coater.  As our test
> substrates are extremely thin and fragile, we cannot use them on headway
> for coating.  We will use headway only as a reference on thick samples.
>
> Below is the process we propose:
>
> 1. Coat PRM2150 using micromist coater or headway (I have attached the
> updated PROM application for Solip PRM2150).
> 2. Softbake 40-50C on hotplate
> 3. Coat Solip Flex9H using hand held wire coater or headway.  (I have
> attached the updated PROM application for Solip Flex9H).
> 4. Complete the curing process.
>
> I hope this is an acceptable process flow. Please let me know if there are
> any questions on this.
>
> In the event, Mitch replies that we are okay to use Solip Flex9H on the
> micromist coater, I was hoping to still pursue it as an option.
>
> Please let me know if there are any questions on this.  Looking forward to
> your response on this.
>
> Best,
> Amit
>

> micro mist coater.  But we are also looking for wire bar coaters for "0um"
> thickness and will use the wire coater approach in case the micro mist
> coater approach is not successful.

On Tue, Jul 14, 2020 at 10:53 AM Michelle Rincon <michelle.rincon at gmail.com<mailto:michelle.rincon at gmail.com>> wrote:
Hi Amit,

Thank you for your patience!

We have some follow up questions for you:
1. As far as the Solip Flex9H:
-NTE does not recommend using the mist coater, so we are not going to allow it.
-Your proposal for the Headway is approved.
-As for the wire coating, have you done this procedure before? Where would you propose to do it? It definitely would fit within the safety specifications, but we should work out the logistics.
No, we have not used this process before.  We propose to use the wire bar coater in the same hood as headway2.  We will clean up and dispose of the wipes, solvents etc. in ziplock bags and then into the solvent waste receptacles.

2. For the 2150,
- Mitch and NTE do have some concerns that it won't spray well since it is water based. You may need to try mixing it with NMP to see if that might help. You are approved to try that on the mister if you wish, but you will need to make sure that you very carefully and thoroughly clean the coater with IPA when you are finished and to collect the cleanroom wipes separately for disposal.
- Your request for the headway is approved.
- Do you think the wire coater might work for this too?
The primer needs to be as thin as possible so we were hoping to use the micro mist coater.  But we are also looking for wire bar coaters for "0um" thickness and will use the wire coater approach in case the micro mist coater approach is not successful.

Best regards,
Michelle

On Thu, Jul 9, 2020 at 8:54 AM Amit Gupta <agupta66 at gmail.com<mailto:agupta66 at gmail.com>> wrote:
Hi Michelle and SNF PROM Committee,

I would like to propose a change to the flow process to address the safety concern of using Solip Flex9H on the micromist coater.

I see that the primer, Solip PRM 2150, is non-flammable from the MSDS and so I think it should be okay to use with the micromist coater.  As our test substrates are extremely thin and fragile, we cannot use them on headway for coating.  We will use headway only as a reference on thick samples.

Below is the process we propose:

1. Coat PRM2150 using micromist coater or headway (I have attached the updated PROM application for Solip PRM2150).
2. Softbake 40-50C on hotplate
3. Coat Solip Flex9H using hand held wire coater or headway.  (I have attached the updated PROM application for Solip Flex9H).
4. Complete the curing process.

I hope this is an acceptable process flow. Please let me know if there are any questions on this.

In the event, Mitch replies that we are okay to use Solip Flex9H on the micromist coater, I was hoping to still pursue it as an option.

Please let me know if there are any questions on this.  Looking forward to your response on this.

Best,
Amit

From mtang at stanford.edu  Tue Jul 21 06:42:35 2020
From: mtang at stanford.edu (Mary Tang)
Date: Tue, 21 Jul 2020 06:42:35 -0700
Subject: [snf-promcommittee] chemicals for PROM approval
In-Reply-To: <CAE7c8nCRJHA88UTtZQYvCyDNs6qu40g7TeAsMLSYJvsTA0-6qA@mail.gmail.com>

Message-ID: <b668df79-aac6-71b3-e802-9a078ab4460a@stanford.edu>

Hi all -

I think the chemicals here are fine.? My question would be more of the 
logistics.? It looks like they are building a DNA resequencing chip of 
some sort, so another question would be what kind of time in the lab is 
needed -- how many oligos and how many Heidelberg cycles?

1.? Will chemicals be brought in each time?

2.? I'm assuming some bio tools will be needed (micropipetters, 
microfuge tubes, possibly vortex mixer).? This means bench top space to 
work.? It would be good to include which one, etc. in his protocol.

3.? Likewise, if it is a cyclic process, extensive time off and on the 
Heidelberg will be needed.? It would be good to have an estimate - and 
if needed, encourage off-hours use.

4.? How and where will S1318 and MF321 be brought in and stored? 
Ideally, as commercial products, they should be shipped here from the 
manufacturer.? We want to discourage people from transporting their own 
hazardous chemicals (the bio stuff I have not an issue with).

Just my initial thoughts.? Think we could persuade him to write up a 
more integrated and detailed protocol?

Mary

From mmrincon at stanford.edu  Tue Jul 21 16:44:42 2020
From: mmrincon at stanford.edu (Michelle Rincon)
Date: Tue, 21 Jul 2020 16:44:42 -0700
Subject: [snf-promcommittee] chemicals for PROM approval
In-Reply-To: <CAE7c8nCRJHA88UTtZQYvCyDNs6qu40g7TeAsMLSYJvsTA0-6qA@mail.gmail.com>

Message-ID: <CAFSuyDTDfbk8mdJVxkTc8PaqCZPUvHk0YdK+99J4NLic+GXMMA@mail.gmail.com>

Hi Alex,

It was nice to see you at the Process Clinic today! As we mentioned- it
would very much help us if you could put together a step by step plan for
what exactly you would like to do and at which location in the lab. Some of
the details we would like you to address are:
- Will you be mixing things within the cleanroom? The DNA MSDS lists that
it is a powder- powders are not allowed in the cleanroom, for example, so
if it is not in solution when you bring it in, we will have to find
somewhere else for you to use it.
- Would you also please clarify any labware that you may expect to use and
if you will be supplying your own or if you think it is something we may
have?
- You also mentioned that you are not planning to store anything at SNF- is
your plan to bring them with you each time you use them? Some of your
materials are hazardous and we would want to make sure that your transport
plan is safe- otherwise we can try and store them in the lab.
- Could you provide some estimate of scheduling? The Heidelberg is a very
busy tool and we are operating under Occupancy restrictions so a little
planning around the scheduling details will be helpful.

Thanks!
Michelle

On Tue, Jul 14, 2020 at 10:53 AM Michelle Rincon <michelle.rincon at gmail.com<mailto:michelle.rincon at gmail.com>> wrote:

Hi Amit,



Thank you for your patience!



We have some follow up questions for you:

1. As far as the Solip Flex9H:

-NTE does not recommend using the mist coater, so we are not going to allow it.

-Your proposal for the Headway is approved.

-As for the wire coating, have you done this procedure before? Where would you propose to do it? It definitely would fit within the safety specifications, but we should work out the logistics.

No, we have not used this process before.  We propose to use the wire bar coater in the same hood as headway2.  We will clean up and dispose of the wipes, solvents etc. in ziplock bags and then into the solvent waste receptacles.



2. For the 2150,

- Mitch and NTE do have some concerns that it won't spray well since it is water based. You may need to try mixing it with NMP to see if that might help. You are approved to try that on the mister if you wish, but you will need to make sure that you very carefully and thoroughly clean the coater with IPA when you are finished and to collect the cleanroom wipes separately for disposal.

- Your request for the headway is approved.

- Do you think the wire coater might work for this too?

The primer needs to be as thin as possible so we were hoping to use the micro mist coater.  But we are also looking for wire bar coaters for "0um" thickness and will use the wire coater approach in case the micro mist coater approach is not successful.



Best regards,

Michelle



On Thu, Jul 9, 2020 at 8:54 AM Amit Gupta <agupta66 at gmail.com<mailto:agupta66 at gmail.com>> wrote:

Hi Michelle and SNF PROM Committee,



I would like to propose a change to the flow process to address the safety concern of using Solip Flex9H on the micromist coater.



I see that the primer, Solip PRM 2150, is non-flammable from the MSDS and so I think it should be okay to use with the micromist coater.  As our test substrates are extremely thin and fragile, we cannot use them on headway for coating.  We will use headway only as a reference on thick samples.



Below is the process we propose:



1. Coat PRM2150 using micromist coater or headway (I have attached the updated PROM application for Solip PRM2150).

2. Softbake 40-50C on hotplate

3. Coat Solip Flex9H using hand held wire coater or headway.  (I have attached the updated PROM application for Solip Flex9H).

4. Complete the curing process.



I hope this is an acceptable process flow. Please let me know if there are any questions on this.



In the event, Mitch replies that we are okay to use Solip Flex9H on the micromist coater, I was hoping to still pursue it as an option.



Please let me know if there are any questions on this.  Looking forward to your response on this.



Best,

Amit

Message-ID: <CAE7c8nBkOv8k9SLQboXP3aasavVj9NV6UPYitB8q3nSeUvQX9A@mail.gmail.com>

Hi Michelle,

It was great to attend the clinic yesterday. Very helpful. Thanks for all
the great suggestions.

To help SNF better understand the experiment process we are planning, the
attachment is a detailed step by step protocol, including the equipment we
are planning to use, the time and the labware. To quickly summarize them
here and answer your questions:

1. we will not mix anything in the cleanroom. All the biology reagents will
be in aqueous solutions as indicated in the PROM application.

2. we will not use labware from SNF. We will bring clean glass troughs for
our experiment to eliminate any contamination. See the attachment for a
detailed list.

3. We will follow the guidelines of SNF to transport chemicals to ensure
safety. For biology aqueous solutions, we will need to keep them on ice and
bring them into the lab. They will be in very small volume (10~20mL) tubes.
For photoresist, I am wondering what the best way is to transfer them into
the lab? I remember there was a recommended procedure during the training
but it will be great to have a refreshment. It will be great if we can
temporarily store them overnight while we are doing this 2-day experiment.

4. we plan to do this 2-day experiment on a weekend during mid-late August
and hope to avoid the traffic. It will take about 8 hours each day
following the protocol.

Please let me know any questions you may have.

Thanks,

Alex

>
> I would like to propose a change to the flow process to address the safety
> concern of using Solip Flex9H on the micromist coater.
>
>
>
> I see that the primer, Solip PRM 2150, is non-flammable from the MSDS and
> so I think it should be okay to use with the micromist coater.  As our test
> substrates are extremely thin and fragile, we cannot use them on headway
> for coating.  We will use headway only as a reference on thick samples.
>
>
>
> Below is the process we propose:
>
>
>
> 1. Coat PRM2150 using micromist coater or headway (I have attached the
> updated PROM application for Solip PRM2150).
>
> 2. Softbake 40-50C on hotplate
>
> 3. Coat Solip Flex9H using hand held wire coater or headway.  (I have
> attached the updated PROM application for Solip Flex9H).
>
> 4. Complete the curing process.
>
>
>
> I hope this is an acceptable process flow. Please let me know if there are
> any questions on this.
>
>
>
> In the event, Mitch replies that we are okay to use Solip Flex9H on the
> micromist coater, I was hoping to still pursue it as an option.

>> safety. For biology aqueous solutions, we will need to keep them on ice and
>> bring them into the lab. They will be in very small volume (10~20mL) tubes.
>> For photoresist, I am wondering what the best way is to transfer them into
>> the lab? I remember there was a recommended procedure during the training
>> but it will be great to have a refreshment. It will be great if we can
>> temporarily store them overnight while we are doing this 2-day experiment.
>>
>> 4. we plan to do this 2-day experiment on a weekend during mid-late
>> August and hope to avoid the traffic. It will take about 8 hours each day
>> following the protocol.
>>
>> Please let me know any questions you may have.
>>
>> Thanks,

>>> Hi Alex,
>>>
>>> It was nice to see you at the Process Clinic today! As we mentioned- it
>>> would very much help us if you could put together a step by step plan for
>>> what exactly you would like to do and at which location in the lab. Some of
>>> the details we would like you to address are:
>>> - Will you be mixing things within the cleanroom? The DNA MSDS lists
>>> that it is a powder- powders are not allowed in the cleanroom, for example,
>>> so if it is not in solution when you bring it in, we will have to find
>>> somewhere else for you to use it.
>>> - Would you also please clarify any labware that you may expect to use
>>> and if you will be supplying your own or if you think it is something we
>>> may have?
>>> - You also mentioned that you are not planning to store anything at SNF-
>>> is your plan to bring them with you each time you use them? Some of your
>>> materials are hazardous and we would want to make sure that your transport
>>> plan is safe- otherwise we can try and store them in the lab.
>>> - Could you provide some estimate of scheduling? The Heidelberg is a
>>> very busy tool and we are operating under Occupancy restrictions so a
>>> little planning around the scheduling details will be helpful.
>>>
>>> Thanks!
